\begin{itemize}
\item Rung 3: the period-8 stripe orbit of Table~\ref{tab:orbits}. At $\alpha=0.071674$ and $\log_{10}T=-0.115$,
  read as exact binary64 rationals (the temperature is the real number $\exp(\ln 10\cdot\log_{10}T)$ for that exact
  dyadic $\log_{10}T$), the expected-embedding map of the stored Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct checkpoint (all 24 layers, the
  full 151\,936-token readout, the fixed 37-token prefix, steering after zero-based block 12) has a primitive
  period-8 orbit, which is the unique multiple-shooting root in a product of eight boxes of coordinate radius
  $2^{-640}$ around refined centers, and the orbit is locally attracting. Ball arithmetic (Arb
  \cite{johansson2017arb}) encloses the map and its Jacobian over each box ($8\times896$ directions); a Krawczyk-type
  inclusion \cite{krawczyk1969newton,moore2009interval,rump2010verification} for the preconditioned shooting system gives existence and uniqueness, separation of the first point
  from the points one, two and four steps later gives the primitive period, and a norm bound on powers of the
  enclosed monodromy gives attraction. The map is the real-number map of the stored constants, read as exact
  dyadic rationals: rotary angles, exponentials and square roots are real functions enclosed by the balls, not
  floating-point tables. An independent audit in exact rational arithmetic rechecked the saved witnesses (the
  scalar inequalities, the primitive-period separations and the row norms of the saved monodromy powers); it did
  not recompute the enclosures of the model or the dense matrix products, which rest on one execution. The proof
  trusts the Python, FLINT and Arb stack and the stored weights. It does not exclude other period-8 points in the
  first box, and it says nothing about the window's boundaries or global organization. The record
  (\path{certificates/llm-p8-certificate-final-001.json}) pins the SHA-256 hashes of the checkpoint, its
  configuration and the steering vector, and lists the 37 prefix token IDs.
\item Rung 3: coexistence. At $\alpha=0.06468$ and $\log_{10}T=-0.148$, inside the strip of the crescent where the
  2-cycle and the 3-cycle both exist (Section~\ref{sec:picture}), the same map, with the same reading of
  parameters and constants, has a primitive period-2 orbit and a primitive period-3 orbit. Each is the unique
  multiple-shooting root in its product of boxes of coordinate radius $2^{-640}$ around refined centers, and each is
  locally attracting; different primitive periods make them distinct orbits. The construction is that of the
  period-8 proof. The point enclosures are computed at 2048 bits and the derivative enclosures at 1024 bits, in 70
  pieces that cover every phase and all 896 directions (28 for the 2-cycle, 42 for the 3-cycle; 37.5 worker-hours
  on rented CPUs). Krawczyk-type inclusion gives existence and local uniqueness, and separation from the proper
  divisors gives the primitive periods. For attraction, the unweighted bounds on the first and second powers of
  the enclosed monodromy failed the sufficient condition: the monodromy is strongly nonnormal, so a single norm
  overstates its growth. These failed attempts are kept in the record, and the fourth and eighth powers pass. Their
  induced infinity-norm bounds, rounded up, are $7.51\times10^{-4}$ and $1.9\times10^{-8}$ for the 2-cycle and
  $0.342$ and $1.091\times10^{-3}$ for the 3-cycle; the numerical leading multiplier moduli are $0.0476$ and $0.239$. An
  independent exact-rational audit rechecked every saved row norm (1\,605\,632 entries per orbit) and the scalar
  and separation witnesses. A separate check certifies the token loops: over every box of both orbits, the most probable next token leads all
  151\,936 tokens by a positive margin, so the 2-cycle writes `` is it'' and the 3-cycle a blank line, ``The'' and
  `` existence'' (\path{codex/coexistence-task33/token-labels-release.json}); the same check gives the period-8
  orbit's loop `` is there is a is a. This''. These are the readouts of the feedback orbits, not sentences generated
  autoregressively. A further certificate gives explicit basin neighborhoods: every state in the closed
  infinity-norm ball of radius $2^{-681}$ about the 2-cycle's frozen center at phase 0 converges to the 2-cycle, and
  every state within $2^{-700}$ of the 3-cycle's center converges to the 3-cycle
  (\path{codex/coexistence-task33/inner-basin-release.json}). These neighborhoods are tiny. The proofs say nothing
  about which orbit a start outside them reaches, including the fixed start of the maps, nor about the trapping of
  the whole box or the window's global organization. The record is
  \path{codex/coexistence-task33/pair-certificate-release.json}.
\item Rung 2: the 2-cycle's fold and its normal form. An independent reimplementation of the forward map and solver
  recovers $\alpha_c$, $a$ and $b$ (to $6\times10^{-15}$, $3\times10^{-12}$ and $2\times10^{-6}$ relatively), and $ab$
  also follows from the stable-branch multipliers. The same reimplementation reproduces all 18 fixed-entrance counts
  of the controlled passage runs exactly (Table~\ref{tab:passages} lists 12 of them).
\item Prospectively frozen: the passage forecasts at two new folds (Table~\ref{tab:prospective}); 23 of 24 within one
  iterate (16 of the 17 longer than five iterates), and the all-row criterion failed by one row. A second frozen test
  at two more folds (Table~\ref{tab:prospective2}): 18 of 24 within one iterate of the primary forecast; it failed at
  the entrance passages of a period-6 fold, short by 3.1 to 5.2\,\%.
\item Rung 2 as well: the period-2 and period-8 orbits of Table~\ref{tab:orbits} (independent implementation), and
  the cascade's first four flip parameters (CPU and H100, agreement $4\times10^{-15}$).
\item Rung 2 as well: the hostile 2-cycle fold and its normal form, reproduced by an independent float64 forward map
  with nested automatic differentiation (location to $6\times10^{-10}$, $ab$ to 8 parts per million); its controlled
  passages were also checked independently, with the frozen all-six criterion failing by one row.
\item Rung 1: the other orbits, the curves, the thirteen located cusps, the superstable parameters,
  the 1.5B flips, the hostile windows' edges, the spiral region's period-7 body and the cascade inside it, the three 7B
  orbits, and Huginn's float32 spectral estimates.
\item Prospectively frozen, not independently reproduced: the scaling predictions P1--P3, the prompt-blocked size
  tests in Qwen2.5 (7B and 14B; 72B; 72B base against Instruct) and OLMo-2, and the loop-free predictor test of Section~\ref{sec:sizemain} and Appendix~\ref{sec:size}, all scored
  by code pushed before the data, together with the three-offset plateau tests of the 70B-class maps and the float64
  recheck of the Qwen2.5-72B cells with automatic-differentiation tangent exponents. These are
  float64 computations at rung 1; no independent implementation has rechecked them.
\item Prospectively frozen on 15 October: the answer-level reproducibility test in Qwen2.5-0.5B (positive), whose
  saved rows and statistics an independent implementation reproduced (the generation itself was not rerun); its 7B
  extension (positive) and the behavior test (null on its primary question; no edge optimum), not independently reproduced. All are reported in Section~\ref{sec:consequences}, the behavior test in detail in Appendix~\ref{sec:behaviour}, and each was scored by code pushed before the data
  (\path{docs/repro-task-test.md}, \path{docs/bpc-test.md}).
\item Prospectively frozen on 18 October, not independently reproduced: the fixed-shape control and dose test in
  Qwen2.5-0.5B (448 cells; \path{docs/fs-design.md}; Section~\ref{sec:consequences}). With unchanged matrix shapes the
  paired runs are bitwise identical in every cell, a changed shape reproduces the earlier comparison, and in the
  chaotic cells the primary dose criterion holds at every dose from $10^{-15}$ to $10^{-7}$. Of the secondary
  criteria, the slope passed and the periodic one failed: from $d=10^{-9}$ on, more than 10\,\% of the periodic
  cells separated.
\item Independently reproduced from saved data (15 October): every summary field of the 0.5B batch-layout comparison
  (448 cells, four comparisons) and the gain-split, logit-bias and crossover summaries of Section~\ref{sec:theory}
  (5353, 6837 and 24 cells); the coefficients and offset rows of the flip normal-form test (Table~\ref{tab:flipnf}); the
  1.5B and 7B fold-law scores under both period selectors (all 2700 cells); and the scalar conditions of the nine
  located cusps, matched one-to-one to the tangent reversals of the seven continued fold curves. This reproduces the
  statistics and saved scalars, not the model runs, derivatives or eigenvectors. The within-prompt steered test (null) used an analysis
  fixed and pushed before its data, without a separate frozen design document.
\item Prospectively frozen and independently replayed: Huginn's relaxation test on held-out data, confirmed in both
  parts; an independent implementation reproduced both from the saved rows and spectra (GSM8K part exactly,
  item-level part to $10^{-12}$) (Section~\ref{sec:huginn}).
\end{itemize}
\paragraph{Hardware.} Float64 GPU computations ran on rented NVIDIA H100 (NVL, PCIe and 80\,GB HBM3), H200,
H200 NVL and A100 80\,GB (SXM4 and PCIe) GPUs, one GPU per run except for the 70B-class models, whose layers were
spread over two; the float32 frames of the rotation video ran on RTX 4090 GPUs; the first reset test of Section~\ref{sec:consequences} ran on a local NVIDIA RTX 2000 Ada workstation GPU and its replication on an H100 NVL; cross-checks, the interval
proof, the second prospective passage test, the coexistence runs and the near-nilpotent searches ran on CPUs. The manifest
\path{docs/manifests/runpod-runs.json} records for each run the GPU type, the code commit, the job and its
arguments, the wall time and the exit status, and the available hashes of the uploaded code and input archives and of
the retrieved results, with absent fields left explicit; per-run receipts and accepted provenance limitations are
kept with it. Regenerated at 02:11 UTC on 16 October 2026, it covers the runs up to that time: it lists 438 runs with retrieved results (code and
input-archive hashes for 330, result-archive hashes for 412) and 21 pods that ended without results. Later runs are
recorded with their receipts in the research log. GPU types round float64 reductions differently, so quantities used across runs
were checked across hardware where it mattered (for example, flip parameters agree between CPU and H100 to
$4\times10^{-15}$).
